### Vilnius District Municipality

Further information: [Vilnius District Municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_District_Municipality)

Aerial view of an old fortress

[Medininkai Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medininkai_Castle), built in the first half of the 14th century. It is the largest enclosure-type defensive [castle in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_castles_in_Lithuania) and a primary landmark in the district.

[^cite_note-168]

See caption

Map of Vilnius district

Vilnius District Municipality (Lithuanian: *Vilniaus rajono savivaldybė*), one of the country's largest municipalities, covers 2,129 square kilometres (822 sq mi) and has [23 elderships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_District_Municipality#Elderships). There are over 1,000 villages and five towns ([Nemenčinė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nemen%C4%8Din%C4%97), [Bezdonys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bezdonys), [Maišiagala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mai%C5%A1iagala), [Mickūnai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mick%C5%ABnai) and [Šumskas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0umskas)) in the district. It borders [Belarus](3457.md) and the [Švenčionys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0ven%C4%8Dionys_district), [Moletai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mol%C4%97tai_district), [Širvintos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0irvintos_district), [Elektrėnai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elektr%C4%97nai_district), [Trakai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trakai_district) and [Šalčininkai districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0al%C4%8Dininkai_district).[^cite_note-district-169]

The district has a multinational population, of which 52 percent are [Poles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poles_in_Lithuania), 33 percent [Lithuanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanians), and the remainder [Russians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russians_in_Lithuania), [Belarusians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belarusians_in_Lithuania) and other nationalities (including [Ukrainians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainians_in_Lithuania)). It has a population of over 100,000; 95 percent live in villages, and five percent live in towns.[^cite_note-district-169] Vilnius district has Lithuania's highest terrain, with the [Aukštojas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auk%C5%A1tojas_Hill), [Juozapinė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juozapin%C4%97_Hill) and [Kruopinė Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kruopin%C4%97_Hill) over 290 metres (950 ft) above [sea level](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_level).[^cite_note-district-169]

[Palm Sunday](24178.md) is celebrated in the district, and Vilnian Easter palms (*verbos*) are made from dried flowers and herbs.[^cite_note-170] Palm-making dates to the time of St. Casimir.[^cite_note-district-169]

[Medininkai Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medininkai_Castle), the [Liubavas Manor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liubavas_Manor) mill and Bareikiškės Manor are the district's best-known historic landmarks.[^cite_note-district-169] From 1769 to 1795, [Vilnius Voivodeship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Voivodeship) surrounded the independent [Republic of Paulava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Paulava). The [microstate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_microstates), known for its [Enlightenment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_Enlightenment) values, had its own president, [peasant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peasant) parliament, army and laws.[^cite_note-171]

With its large Polish population, the Vilnius District Municipality Council primarily consists of members of the [Electoral Action of Poles in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electoral_Action_of_Poles_in_Lithuania) party.[^cite_note-172] Its mayor is [Robert Duchniewicz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Duchniewicz) of the [Lithuanian Social Democratic Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Social_Democratic_Union).[^cite_note-173]

### National government



[Seimas Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seimas_Palace) in Vilnius, where the [parliamentarians of Lithuania](571739.md) convenes

A large room with horseshoe-shaped seating, seen from above

A Seimas [plenary session](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plenary_session)

Vilnius is the seat of Lithuania's [national government](16769373.md). The country's two chief officers have their offices in Vilnius. The [president](273654.md)'s seat is at the [Presidential Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Palace,_Vilnius) at [Daukanto Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daukanto_Square),[^cite_note-174] and the [prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Lithuania)'s seat is at the Government of Lithuania office on Gediminas Avenue.[^cite_note-175] Both the president has a residence in Vilnius' Turniškės district near the Neris.[^cite_note-176][^cite_note-177] The prime minister is also has entitled to a residence in Turniškės district during their term in office.[^cite_note-178] Government ministries are located throughout the city, many in the Old Town.[^cite_note-179]

The [Seimas](571739.md) of the Grand Duchy of Lithuania primarily gathered in Vilnius.[^cite_note-180] The present-day Seimas meets at the [Seimas Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seimas_Palace) on Gediminas Avenue.[^cite_note-181]

Lithuania's highest courts are in Vilnius. The [Supreme Court of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Lithuania) (Lithuanian: *Lietuvos Aukščiausiasis Teismas*), which reviews criminal and civil cases, is on Gynėjų Street.[^cite_note-182] The [Supreme Administrative Court of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Administrative_Court_of_Lithuania) (Lithuanian: *Lietuvos vyriausiasis administracinis teismas*), which adjudicates litigation against public bodies, is on Žygimantų Street.[^cite_note-183] The [Constitutional Court of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Lithuania) (Lithuanian: *Lietuvos Respublikos Konstitucinis Teismas*), an advisory body with authority over the constitutionality of laws, meets in the Constitutional Court Palace on Gediminas Avenue.[^cite_note-184] The [Lithuanian Tribunal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Tribunal), the highest [appellate court](643.md) for the nobility of the Grand Duchy of Lithuania and established by [Stephen Báthory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_B%C3%A1thory) in 1581, was in Vilnius until the [Third Partition of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_Partition_of_Poland) in 1795.[^cite_note-185]

### Special services

A police officer on a motorized scooter

A police officer patrolling with a [Segway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Segway)

Exterior of a large, gray, modern building

The Emergency Response Center in [Antakalnis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antakalnis), which handles emergency calls in Vilnius

Security in Vilnius is primarily the responsibility of the *Vilniaus apskrities vyriausiasis policijos komisariatas*, the highest police office in the city, and local police offices. Its main responsibilities are ensuring public order and safety, reporting and investigating criminal offenses, and traffic control.[^cite_note-186] In 2016, the city had 1,500 police officers.[^cite_note-187] The [Public Security Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Security_Service_\(Lithuania\)) is responsible for the prompt restoration of public order in special situations and ensuring the protection of important state objects and escorted subjects.[^cite_note-188]

*Vilniaus apskrities priešgaisrinė gelbėjimo valdyba* is the primary governing body of Vilnius's [firefighters](146303.md).[^cite_note-189] There were 1,287 fire incidents in the first nine months of 2018, killing six people and injuring 16.[^cite_note-190]

*Vilniaus greitosios medicinos pagalbos stotis* is responsible for [emergency medical services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emergency_medical_services) in the city, and the EMS telephone number is 113.[^cite_note-191] Established in 1902, it is one of eastern Europe's oldest EMS institutions.[^cite_note-vgmps-192] Many doctors and other personnel received medals for their assistance to victims of the 1991 January Events.[^cite_note-vgmps-192] The common number for contacting emergency services in Vilnius and other parts of Lithuania is 112.[^cite_note-193]

## Cityscape

See also: [Vilnius Central Business District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Central_Business_District)

Large photo of Vilnius, seen from above

Panorama of Vilnius Old Town, seen from [Gediminas' Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gediminas'_Tower) at dusk. Vilnius has one of the largest and best-preserved old towns in [northern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Europe), [eastern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Europe), and [central Europe](5188.md).

[^cite_note-unesco-16]

[^cite_note-TheCapital-194]

[^cite_note-VWH-195]

[^cite_note-Lurk-196]

&#x20;Its skyline is dominated by [spires of churches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_churches_in_Vilnius) dating to the Grand Duchy of Lithuania.

[^cite_note-197]

[^cite_note-VWH-195]

### Urbanism and architecture

Exterior of two old, red churches

[St. Anne's Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_St._Anne,_Vilnius) and the [Church of St. Francis and St. Bernard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_St._Francis_and_St._Bernard,_Vilnius) are examples of [Gothic architecture in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gothic_architecture_in_Lithuania).

Interior of a large Baroque church

The [Baroque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baroque_architecture) [Church of St. Peter and St. Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_St._Peter_and_St._Paul,_Vilnius) was built by [Michał Kazimierz Pac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Micha%C5%82_Kazimierz_Pac) to commemorate victory over the Muscovites and their expulsion from Vilnius after six years of occupation.

The Old Town covers about 3.6 km<sup>2</sup> (1.4 sq mi), and its history dates to the [Neolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neolithic). The glacial hills were intermittently occupied, and a wooden castle was built at the confluence of the Neris and Vilnia c. 1000 AD to fortify [Gedimino Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gedimino_Hill). The settlement developed into a town in the 13th century, when the [pagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pagan) [Baltic people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltic_people) were invaded by [Western Europeans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Europeans) during the [Lithuanian Crusade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Crusade). Around 1323 (the first written sources about Vilnia), it was the capital of the Grand Duchy of Lithuania and had a few brick buildings. By the 15th century, the duchy extended from the [Baltic](3335.md) to the [Black Sea](3386.md) (primarily present-day Belarus, Ukraine and Russia). The historic centre consists of [three castles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Castle_Complex) (Upper, Lower and Curved) and the area previously encircled by the [Wall of Vilnius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wall_of_Vilnius). It is mainly circular, centered on the original castle site. Streets are small and narrow, with large squares later developed.[^cite_note-unesco-16] [Pilies Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pilies_Street), the main artery, links the Palace of the Grand Dukes of Lithuania with Vilnius Town Hall. Other streets are lined with the palaces of feudal lords and landlords, churches, shops and craftspeople's workrooms.

Historic buildings feature [Gothic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gothic_architecture),[^cite_note-198] [Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance_architecture),[^cite_note-199] [Baroque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baroque_architecture)[^cite_note-200] and [classical architecture](52687.md).[^cite_note-201] The variety of preserved churches and former palaces of the Lithuanian nobility exemplifies Vilnius's multicultural heritage.[^cite_note-unesco-16][^cite_note-202]

Lithuanians and others shaped the development of the capital, with Western and Eastern influences. Lithuania was [Christianized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianization_of_Lithuania) in 1387, but Eastern Orthodoxy and the growing importance of [Judaism](15624.md) led to construction of the [Orthodox Cathedral of the Theotokos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_of_the_Theotokos,_Vilnius) and the [Great Synagogue of Vilna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Synagogue_of_Vilna)).[^cite_note-unesco-16]

An ornate chapel, seen from the side

The 17th-century Chapel of Saint Casimir, the [patron saint](68055.md) of Lithuania and its youth, in [Vilnius Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Cathedral)

Disasters resulted in buildings reconstructions in the [Vilnian Baroque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnian_Baroque) style, which later influenced the Grand Duchy of Lithuania.[^cite_note-unesco-16][^cite_note-VilniusBaroqueVWH-17][^cite_note-Paknys2021-18] Italian artists such as [Bernardinus Zanobi da Gianotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernardinus_Zanobi_da_Gianotti), [Giovani Cini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovani_Cini) and Swiss–Italian artists such as [Matteo Castelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matteo_Castelli), [Pietro Perti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pietro_Perti), [Costante Tencalla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Costante_Tencalla), [Jacopo Tencalla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacopo_Tencalla) from the present-day [Canton of Ticino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canton_of_Ticino) were preferred by the Grand Duke of Lithuania and local nobility, and designed, decorated the [Palace of the Grand Dukes of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_the_Grand_Dukes_of_Lithuania), [Chapel of Saint Casimir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chapel_of_Saint_Casimir), [Sapieha Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sapieha_Palace,_Vilnius), [Slushko Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slushko_Palace), [Minor Radvilos Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minor_Radvilos_Palace), etc.[^cite_note-Paknys2021-18][^cite_note-203] German descent architect [Johann Christoph Glaubitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johann_Christoph_Glaubitz) also extensively contributed to Baroque style reconstructions of buildings. Lithuanian [Laurynas Gucevičius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laurynas_Gucevi%C4%8Dius) was a noted [Neoclassical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoclassical_architecture) architect in the city.[^cite_note-204]

The 352-hectare (870-acre) Old Town was designated as a [UNESCO World Heritage Site](44940.md) in 1994. The Vilnius Historic Centre is noted for maintaining its medieval street pattern with no significant gaps. Some places were damaged during Lithuania's occupations and wars, including [Cathedral Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_Square,_Vilnius) (demolished in 1795) and a square east of the [Church of All Saints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_All_Saints,_Vilnius) where the [Convent of the Barefoot Carmelites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_St._Joseph_the_Betrothed,_Vilnius) stood with [Vice-Chancellor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deputy_Chancellor_of_Lithuania) [Stefan Pac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefan_Pac)'s Baroque [Church of St. Joseph the Betrothed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_St._Joseph_the_Betrothed,_Vilnius) (both demolished by the [tsar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsar). The Great Synagogue and part of the buildings in [Vokiečių Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vokie%C4%8Di%C5%B3_Street) were demolished after World War II.[^cite_note-unesco-16]

The [Vilnius Central Business District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Central_Business_District) in [Šnipiškės](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0nipi%C5%A1k%C4%97s) neighborhood is developed since the 2000s as mostly [high-rises](118767.md) [central business district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_business_district).[^cite_note-205]

Vilnius covers 401 square kilometres (155 sq mi), of which one-fifth is developed; the remainder is greenspace and water. The city is known as one of Europe's "greenest" capital cities.[^cite_note-206]

### Crypts

Notable Lithuanian Catholics are interred in the crypts of Vilnius Cathedral. Grand Duke [Alexander Jagiellon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Jagiellon), Queen [Elizabeth of Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_of_Austria_\(1436%E2%80%931505\)), [Barbara Radziwiłł](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbara_Radziwi%C5%82%C5%82), and the heart of Grand Duke Władysław IV Vasa are buried at the Royal Mausoleum. These crypts have one of the country's oldest frescoes, painted in the late 14th or early 15th century after Lithuania was Christianized.[^cite_note-207]

### Housing

Pedestrians on a narrow street

Pilies Street has a medieval atmosphere.

Vilnius Old Town (Lithuanian: *Vilniaus senamiestis*), with medieval stone-paved streets, and Užupis have prestigious housing, with [apartments](265567.md) featuring views of iconic churches and urban landmarks (particularly [Gediminas Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gediminas_Tower)), enclosed inner courtyards, high ceilings, attics, non-standard layouts and luxurious interiors;[^cite_note-asa-208] Flats in these neighbourhoods may cost millions of [euros](9472.md).[^cite_note-209] Traffic jams, expensive parking, air pollution, high maintenance costs and limitations on renovation, however, also encourage wealthy Vilnians to buy or build [private houses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_house) in outlying parts of the city such as [Balsiai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balsiai?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Bajorai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bajorai_\(eldership\)?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Pavilnys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pavilnys?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Kalnėnai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaln%C4%97nai?action=edit\&redlink=1) and [Pilaitė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pilait%C4%97) or the nearby [Vilnius District Municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_District_Municipality).[^cite_note-asa-208] Around 21,000 residents live in the Old Town, and 7,000 in Užupis.[^cite_note-Mikrorajonai-210]

A scenic riverside neighborhood, seen from above

Part of the Valakampiai neighborhood in [Antakalnis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antakalnis) on the Neris, seen from [Verkiai Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Verkiai_Palace)



Helios City complex in [Naujamiestis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naujamiestis,_Vilnius) with [shopping mall](235723.md) and apartments

[Valakampiai](https://lt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valakampiai) and [Turniškės](https://lt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turni%C5%A1k%C4%97s) are prestigious neighborhoods, with private houses on large lots surrounded by [pine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scots_pine) forests which are easily accessible from the city centre. Wealthy people and heads of state (such as the president) live there, and most of the larger private houses costs millions of euros.[^cite_note-asa-208][^cite_note-211] Part of the [Žvėrynas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%BDv%C4%97rynas) neighbourhood has luxurious private houses near Vingis Park, but it also has Soviet-era apartment buildings and wooden houses in poor condition.[^cite_note-Mikrorajonai-210][^cite_note-asa-208]

Neighbourhoods around the Old Town (Antakalnis, Žirmūnai, Naujamiestis, and Žvėrynas) have a variety of flats and green space, and are popular with [middle-class](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_class) residents. Wealthier people live in a new construction or renovated Soviet-era apartments.[^cite_note-asa-208] The government is supportive of renovation, and reimburses 30 percent or more of the cost.[^cite_note-212] Poorer residents and low-income [pensioners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pensioner), however, foster [regionalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regionalism_\(politics\)).[^cite_note-213][^cite_note-214]

More-distant neighbourhoods, such as Lazdynai, Karoliniškės, Viršuliškės, [Šeškinė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0e%C5%A1kin%C4%97), Justiniškės, Pašilaičiai, Fabijoniškės and [Naujininkai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naujininkai), have more-affordable housing. Their disadvantages are a longer commute, unrenovated Soviet-era [high-rise buildings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-rise_building), traffic congestion and a shortage of parking spaces near older apartments.[^cite_note-asa-208][^cite_note-215]

19th-century painting of a large church on a river

Mid-19th-century painting of the Chapel of Jesus of Šnipiškės

The [Šnipiškės](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0nipi%C5%A1k%C4%97s) eldership has received significant investment during the 2010s. The area was first mentioned in 1536, when Grand Duke Sigismund I the Old ordered [Ulrich Hosius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulrich_Hosius) to build a wooden bridge over the Neris and a suburb developed around the bridge. That century, a building for [Muscovite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Duchy_of_Moscow) and [Tatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tatars) messengers was built by the magistrate of Vilnius north of Šnipiškės.[^cite_note-216] The Jesuit [Church of St. Raphael the Archangel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_St._Raphael_the_Archangel,_Vilnius) and monastery and housing for wealthy and middle-class townspeople were built in Šnipiškės during the 18th century. Craftspeople lived on the outskirts, where a smoking-pipe factory, sawmills and a small candy factory were built. The 8-hectare (20-acre) Skansenas neighbourhood, west of the Kalvarijų market,[^cite_note-217] has late-19th-century wooden houses. Nearby Piromontas[^cite_note-218] was built at the same time.

During the 1960s, Šnipiškės was renamed the [New City Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Central_Business_District). It had the city's first pedestrian zone and a number of buildings, including the country's largest shopping centre, a large hotel, a planetarium, a museum and a number of ministries of the Lithuanian SSR, were built before 1990.[^cite_note-219][^cite_note-220][^cite_note-221][^cite_note-222][^cite_note-223] Šnipiškės north of [Konstitucijos Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konstitucijos_Avenue) was underdeveloped until the early 2000s, when the new [Vilnius city municipality building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_city_municipality_building) spurred construction of Europa Square with a shopping centre, a 33-story office building and a 27-story apartment building. The former Museum of the Revolution became the National Art Gallery in the late 2000s.[^cite_note-224]

Aerial view of dense riverside housing

Modern housing in Paupys

According to economists, the number of transactions and the housing affordability index reached record highs in 2019 because of increased city-residents' income and slowing price increases for flats.[^cite_note-225] One-fourth of residents 26 to 35 years old still live in homes owned by their parents or other relatives, however, the highest percentage in the Baltic states.[^cite_note-226]

## Demographics

Main article: [Demographics of Vilnius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Vilnius)

Further information: [Demographic history of the Vilnius region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographic_history_of_the_Vilnius_region)

A red-and-blue graph

Vilnius [population pyramid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_pyramid) in 2021

In the eldership of [Vilkpėdė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilkp%C4%97d%C4%97), remnants of a [Magdalenian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magdalenian) settlement were found which date to c. 10,000 BC. Kairėnai, Pūčkoriai and Naujoji Vilnia had large settlements during the first millennium AD.[^cite_note-historyvle-227] The most densely populated area was the confluence of the Neris and Vilnia, which had fortified homesteads.[^cite_note-historyvle-227]

According to some historians, Vilnius could have been a city during the [Kingdom of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Lithuania) times: King [Mindaugas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mindaugas) did not permanently live there, however, may have built Lithuania's first Catholic church for his [coronation](226191.md) there. It is well established, however, that Vilnius existed as a city during the times of [Traidenis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traidenis) and [Vytenis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vytenis). The first mention in the historical sources as a capital in 1323 in the [letters to the Western cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Letters_of_Gediminas) of [Gediminas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gediminas).

It became a multicultural city, with 14th-century sources noting that it consisted of a Great (Lithuanian) city and a [Ruthenian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruthenians) one. By the 16th century, [German](152735.md) merchants, artisans, Jews and [Tatars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tatars) had also settled in Vilnius. During the 16th–17th centuries [Reformation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reformation) and [Counter-Reformation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counter-Reformation), the city's Polish-speaking population began to grow; in the 15th–16th centuries only 5% of Vilnius townspeople acts were written in Polish, however by the middle of the 17th century most writing of townspeople acts was in Polish (53%).[^cite_note-historyvle-227] The city was inhabited by a large number of [Italian](385155.md) and [Swiss](7351032.md) artisans as well and generally all the European nations were presented to an extent (those included [Vilnius University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_University) professors and students among whom there were [French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_people), [Spanish](963152.md), [Swedes](29380512.md) and even some [Croatians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatians) as Tomaš Zdelarius, musicians at the [Palace of the Grand Dukes of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_the_Grand_Dukes_of_Lithuania) or such military servants as [Hungarian](17106358.md) [Gáspár Bekes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%A1sp%C3%A1r_Bekes)). Because of many nations inhabiting the city, in the 16th–18th centuries it was known and nicknamed in Western sources as [Babylon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babylon) of Europe.[^cite_note-S._Bodniak_1930_p._37-4] Nevertheless, even in the 19th century the Lithuanian population, despite the [loss of statehood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_partition), still formed the largest ethnic group in the [Vilnius Governorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Governorate), according to the estimates of [Stanisław Plater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanis%C5%82aw_Plater) (65% in 1825),[^cite_note-228] Mikhail Lebedkin (49.98% in 1861),[^cite_note-229] but per [1897 Russian census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Empire_census) Lithuanian population decreased to only 17.6%.[^cite_note-230] The 1916 German census estimated that the largest ethnic group living in the city of Vilnius in 1916 were Poles (50.2%; 70,629 of 140,840 residents).[^cite_note-231]

By the inter-war period, after the brief [Polish–Lithuanian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish%E2%80%93Lithuanian_War) and the annexation of the so-called [Republic of Central Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Central_Lithuania) by Poland, the population became overwhelmingly Polish with very significant Jewish minority – the 1931 Polish census recorded 65.9% Poles (128,628 residents) and only 0.8% Lithuanians (1,579 residents) in the city of Vilnius.[^cite_note-232] However, the German census of 1 January 1941 provided significantly different estimates of the ethnic composition of residents of the city of Vilnius: Poles (41.89%; 87,855 residents), Lithuanians (24.37%; 51,111 residents).[^cite_note-233] After World War II, the number of ethnic Lithuanians in Vilnius rebounded; however, Lithuanization was soon replaced with [Sovietization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sovietization_of_the_Baltic_states),[^cite_note-diana427-234][^cite_note-Snyder_92-93-235] and the population became a mix of Lithuanians, Russians and Poles. Following independence in 1990, for the first time in modern history Lithuanians became a clear majority, increasing to 63.2 percent in 2011 and 67.4 percent in 2021.[^cite_note-2011census-236][^cite_note-237][^cite_note-238]
