## Economy

Aerial view of the skyline and a bridge

The skyline at dusk, with the [Vilnius Central Business District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Central_Business_District) housing [banks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_banks_in_Lithuania), financial services and businesses headquarters



[Europa Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Europa_Tower), the [tallest building in the Baltics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_buildings_in_the_Baltic_states), is a symbol of modern Vilnius.

Vilnius is Lithuania's economic centre, with a per-capita GDP in the metropolitan area of over [€](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EUR)40,000.[^cite_note-GDP-11] The city's budget reached €1.0 billion in 2022.[^cite_note-247] The average monthly salary in Vilnius is €2,891 ([US\$](18717338.md)3,419.19) (gross) and €1,749 ([US\$](18717338.md)2,068.54) (net).[^cite_note-248]

Aerial view of a modern, oval building

The K29 business centre is the first office in the Baltic states to receive an excellent [BREEAM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BREEAM) rating.

[^cite_note-249]

Lithuania's economic growth has been uneven, with [GDP](12594.md) per capita at nearly 110 percent of the EU average in Vilnius but from 42 to 77 percent in other regions. The country's [convergence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convergence_\(economics\)) is fuelled by two regions (Vilnius and [Kaunas County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaunas_County)) which produce 42 and 20 percent of the national GDP, respectively. From 2014 to 2016, the Vilnius region grew by 4.6 percent.[^cite_note-250]

The supply of new housing in Vilnius and its suburbs has reached post-recession highs, and the stock of unsold apartments in Lithuania's three largest cities has begun to increase since the beginning of 2017. Demand for housing is strong, fuelled by rising wages, benign financial conditions and positive expectations. In the first half of 2018, the number of monthly transactions was the highest since its 2007–2008 peak.[^cite_note-251] Most [foreign direct investment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_direct_investment) and productive public investment in Lithuania is concentrated on Vilnius and Kaunas.[^cite_note-252] Vilnius Industrial Park, 18.5 kilometres from the city, is intended for commercial and industrial use.[^cite_note-253]

### Science and research

An old university courtyard

The Vilnius University Astronomical Observatory

Vilnius resident [Tito Livio Burattini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tito_Livio_Burattini) published *Misura universale* in 1675, in which he first suggested the term [metre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metre) as a unit of length.[^cite_note-254] The [Vilnius University Astronomical Observatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_University_Astronomical_Observatory), established in 1753 at the initiative of [Thomas Zebrowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Zebrowski), was one of Europe's first [observatories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Observatory) and the first in the Polish-Lithuanian Commonwealth.[^cite_note-255] [Marcin Odlanicki Poczobutt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcin_Odlanicki_Poczobutt) led the reconstruction of the observatory, designed by [Marcin Knackfus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcin_Knackfus), from 1770 to 1772. Poczobutt began his [astronomical observations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Observational_astronomy) in 1773, recording them in the journal ([French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_language): *Cahiers des observations*), and created the constellation *[Taurus Poniatovii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taurus_Poniatovii)*.[^cite_note-256] [Jean-Emmanuel Gilibert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Emmanuel_Gilibert) established the [Botanical Garden of Vilnius University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Botanical_Garden_of_Vilnius_University) in 1781 with over 2,000 plants, and provided the first [herbariums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herbarium), collections of stuffed animals and birds, [fossil plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paleobotany), animal remains, and a collection of [minerals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minerals) to Vilnius University.[^cite_note-257] The observatory published the Russian Empire's first [exact sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exact_sciences) journal, the *Journal of Mathematical Sciences* (Russian: Вестник математических наук), after the Third Partition of Poland.[^cite_note-Klimka-122]

Aerial view of modern buildings and green space

Scientific centres and university faculties at Sunrise Valley

Sunrise Valley Science and Technology Park (Lithuanian: *Saulėtekio slėnio mokslo ir technologijų parkas*) is a non-profit organization which was founded in 2003. Over 20,000 students study in the Vilnius University and [Vilnius Gediminas Technical University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Gediminas_Technical_University) facilities in Sunrise Valley, and 5,000 scientists conduct research in its science centres.[^cite_note-258]

The Centre for Physical Sciences and Technology (Lithuanian: *Fizinių ir technologijos mokslų centras*, FTMC) is the country's largest scientific research institution, specialising in [laser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laser) technology, [optoelectronics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Optoelectronics), [nuclear physics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_physics), [organic chemistry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organic_chemistry), [bio](4502.md) and [nanotechnology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanotechnology), electrochemical [materials science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Materials_science), and [electronics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronics). The centre was created in 2010 with the merger of the institutes of chemistry, physics and semiconductor physics in Vilnius and the Textile institute in Kaunas.[^cite_note-259] With 250 laboratories (24 open to the public), it can accommodate over 700 researchers and students.[^cite_note-260] The centre has a [PhD program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doctor_of_Philosophy) and hosts annual conferences of PhD students and young researchers.[^cite_note-261] FTMC is the founder and sole shareholder of the Science and Technology Park of Institute of Physics in Savanorių Avenue, which assists companies with [research and development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Research_and_development).[^cite_note-262]

Vilnius University's Laser Research Centre (Lithuanian: *Vilniaus universiteto Lazerinių tyrimų centras*) is one of five departments in the university's Faculty of Physics, which prepares physicists, laser physicists and laser-technology specialists. The department conducts research in [laser physics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laser_physics), [nonlinear optics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nonlinear_optics), optical-component characterization, [biophotonics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biophotonics) and laser [microtechnology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microtechnology).[^cite_note-263] Lithuania has over 50 percent of the world's market share in [ultrashort pulses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ultrashort_pulse) lasers produced by Vilnius-based companies.[^cite_note-laser-264] A laser system was produced in 2019 for the [Extreme Light Infrastructure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extreme_Light_Infrastructure) laboratory in [Szeged](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Szeged) which produces high-intensity, ultra-short pulses with a peak power up to 1,000 times that of the most powerful [nuclear power plant](207347.md) in the United States.[^cite_note-laser-264] [Corning Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corning_Inc.) bought a glass-cutting licence from the Vilnius-based laser company Altechna and for manufacturing [Gorilla Glass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gorilla_Glass).[^cite_note-265]

A smiling, bespectacled man speaking into a microphone

Virginijus Šikšnys is a biochemist at Vilnius University.

The Vilnius University Life Sciences Centre (Lithuanian: *Vilniaus universiteto Gyvybės mokslų centras*) is a scientific research centre which consists of three institutes: the Institute of Biochemistry, Institute of Biosciences, and Institute of Biotechnology. The centre was opened in 2016 and has 800 students, 120 PhD students, 200 teaching staff, and open-access scientific laboratories with advanced equipment.[^cite_note-266] It has a technology [business incubator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_incubator) for small and medium businesses in the life sciences or related fields.[^cite_note-267] Vilnius Gediminas Technical University has three research centres at Sunrise Valley: the Civil Engineering Research Centre, Technology Centre for Building Information and Digital Modelling, and Competence Centre of Intermodal Transport and Logistics.[^cite_note-268]

The Lithuanian Centre for Social Sciences (Lithuanian: *Lietuvos socialinių mokslų centras*), which cooperates with the Lithuanian government, produces and disseminates scientific information in the fields of economics, sociology and law to implement public policy.[^cite_note-269] Santara Valley (Lithuanian: *Santaros slėnis*) is a science and research facility which focuses on [medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medicine), [biopharmaceutical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biopharmaceutical) and [bioinformatics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bioinformatics).[^cite_note-270] The [Vilnius University Faculty of Medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_University_Faculty_of_Medicine) Science Centre was scheduled for completion in Santara Valley in 2021.[^cite_note-271]

Vilnius University rector [Jonas Kubilius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonas_Kubilius), known for [probabilistic number theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Probabilistic_number_theory), the [Kubilius model](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kubilius_model), the Theorem of Kubilius and [Turán–Kubilius inequality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tur%C3%A1n%E2%80%93Kubilius_inequality), successfully resisted attempts to Russify Vilnius University.[^cite_note-272] Vilnius's [Marija Gimbutas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marija_Gimbutas) was the first to formulate the [Kurgan hypothesis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurgan_hypothesis). In 1963, [Vytautas Straižys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vytautas_Strai%C5%BEys) and his colleagues created the [Vilnius photometric system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_photometric_system) used in [astronomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astronomy).[^cite_note-273] [Kavli Prize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kavli_Prize) laureate [Virginijus Šikšnys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginijus_%C5%A0ik%C5%A1nys) invented [CRISPR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CRISPR)-[Cas9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cas9) [genetic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gene) editing.[^cite_note-274]

### Information technology

Aerial view of modern buildings

The Green Hall business complex in [Žvėrynas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%BDv%C4%97rynas), which houses IT companies and Europe's first international Blockchain Centre

Vilnius is attractive for foreign companies because of its qualified employees and good infrastructure.[^cite_note-275] Several schools are preparing skilled specialists, including the [Vilnius University Faculty of Mathematics and Informatics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_University_Faculty_of_Mathematics_and_Informatics) and [Vilnius Gediminas Technical University Faculty of Fundamental Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Gediminas_Technical_University_Faculty_of_Fundamental_Sciences).[^cite_note-276][^cite_note-277] [Information technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Information_technology) jobs are well-paid.[^cite_note-278] The 2018 output of the Lituanian IT sector was €2.296 billion, much of which was created in Vilnius.[^cite_note-279]

Vilnius Tech Park in Sapieha Park, the largest IT [startup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Startup) hub in the Baltic and [Nordic countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic_countries), unites international startups, technology companies, accelerators, and incubators.[^cite_note-280] *[fDi Intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FDi_Intelligence)* ranked Vilnius number one city on its 2019 Tech Start-up FDI Attraction Index.[^cite_note-281]

Vilnius had the world's fastest internet speed in 2011[^cite_note-282] and, despite its fall in the rankings, remains one of the world's fastest.[^cite_note-283] [Vilnius Čiurlionis International Airport](166489.md) has one of Europe's fastest airport [Wi-Fi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wi-Fi) speeds.[^cite_note-284] The [National Cyber Security Centre of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Cyber_Security_Centre_of_Lithuania) was established in Vilnius to address internet attacks on Lithuanian government organizations.[^cite_note-285]

Lithuanian[^cite_note-l143-286] company Nord Security, famous for their virtual private network service [NordVPN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NordVPN) has also established their company and offices in Vilnius.[^cite_note-h839-287][^cite_note-v068-288]

[Bebras](https://lt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bebras%20\(konkursas\)), an international informatics and IT contest, has been held annually for pupils in grades three through 12 since 2004.[^cite_note-289] Since 2017, [computer programming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computer_programming) is taught in primary schools.[^cite_note-290]

Vilnius is a popular [fintech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fintech) hub due to Lithuania's flexible [e-money](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E-money) licence regulations.[^cite_note-291] The Bank of Lithuania granted an e-money licence in 2018 to Vilnius-based Google Payment Lithuania.[^cite_note-292] The startup [Revolut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolut) also has an e-money licence and headquarters in Vilnius, and began moving its clients to the Lithuanian company Revolut Payments in 2019.[^cite_note-293] On 23 January 2019, Europe's first international blockchain centre opened in Vilnius.[^cite_note-294]

### Finance and banking

Aerial view of an old, square building with trees in front

[Bank of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_Lithuania) headquarters in [Gediminas Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gediminas_Avenue)

Vilnius is Lithuania's financial centre. The [Ministry of Finance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Finance_\(Lithuania\)) in Vilnius is responsible for an effective public financial policy to ensure the country's economic growth.[^cite_note-295] The [Bank of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_Lithuania) fosters a reliable financial system and ensures sustainable economic growth.[^cite_note-296] The [Nasdaq Vilnius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasdaq_Vilnius) stock exchange is in the K29 business centre.[^cite_note-297]

The [National Audit Office of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Audit_Office_of_Lithuania) (Lithuanian: *Lietuvos Respublikos valstybės kontrolė*) helps the government manage public funds and property,[^cite_note-298] and the [State Tax Inspectorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Tax_Inspectorate) (Lithuanian: *Valstybinė mokesčių inspekcija*) is responsible for collecting and refunding [taxes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxation_in_Lithuania).[^cite_note-299]

In 2023, 13 [banks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_banks_in_Lithuania) held a bank or specialised-bank licence; six banks are foreign-bank branches. Most of the Lithuanian financial system consists of capital banks of Nordic countries.[^cite_note-300] The two largest banks registered in Lithuania ([SEB bankas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SEB_bankas) and Swedbank) are supervised by the [European Central Bank](9474.md) and the Bank of Lithuania.[^cite_note-301]

## Education

### Primary and secondary education

Exterior of a large building with steps and columns

The National M. K. Čiurlionis School of Art offers free education to talented students.

Primary and lower secondary education is mandatory in Lithuania. Children begin pre-primary education at age six, education is compulsory until age 16. Primary and secondary education is free, but there are also private schools in Vilnius. The country's educational system is governed by the [Ministry of Education, Science and Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Education_and_Science_\(Lithuania\)), headquartered in Vilnius.[^cite_note-302]

[Cathedral School of Vilnius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_School_of_Vilnius), first mentioned in a 1397 source, is the earliest known Lithuanian school.[^cite_note-historyvle-227] [Vilnius Vytautas the Great Gymnasium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Vytautas_the_Great_Gymnasium), established in 1915, is the first Lithuanian [gymnasium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gymnasium_\(school\)) in eastern Lithuania.[^cite_note-303] In 2018, the city had 120 schools (not including [preschools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preschool)) with 61,123 pupils and 4,955 teachers.[^cite_note-304] Four out of five best rated schools in Lithuania are in Vilnius, and the [Vilnius Lyceum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Lyceum) is number one.[^cite_note-305]

[Ethnic minorities in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_minorities_in_Lithuania) have their own schools. Vilnius has seven elementary schools, eight primary schools, two [progymnasiums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gymnasium_\(school\)#School_structure) and 12 gymnasiums for minority children, with lessons in minority languages. In 2017, 4,658 Poles and 9,274 Russians studied in their languages in the city.[^cite_note-306] Vilnius has 11 [vocational schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vocational_school).[^cite_note-307]

The [National M. K. Čiurlionis School of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_M._K._%C4%8Ciurlionis_School_of_Art) is the country's only 12-year art school. The [Vilnius Justinas Vienožinskis Art School](https://lt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilniaus%20Justino%20Vieno%C5%BEinskio%20dail%C4%97s%20mokykla) is another art school in Vilnius.

Most school graduates in Vilnius later study at universities or colleges. According to the [OECD](33853117.md), 57.5 percent of 25– to 34-year-olds in Lithuania had a tertiary education in 2021.[^cite_note-308] Vilnius has nine international schools, including the International School of Vilnius, [Vilnius International French Lyceum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_International_French_Lyceum), British International School of Vilnius, and American International School of Vilnius.[^cite_note-309]

### Tertiary education

Old buildings and a church

The Great Courtyard of [Vilnius University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_University) and the [Church of St. Johns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_St._Johns,_Vilnius)

On 14 October 1773, the [Commission of National Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commission_of_National_Education) (Lithuanian: *Edukacinė komisija*) was created by the Sejm of the Polish–Lithuanian Commonwealth and Grand Duke Stanisław August Poniatowski, who supervised schools and Vilnius University in the Commonwealth. Because of its authority and autonomy, it is considered Europe's first [ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_\(government_department\)) of education and an example of the [Enlightenment in the Commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Enlightenment).[^cite_note-310]

Vilnius has a number of universities, the largest and oldest of which is Vilnius University.[^cite_note-Facts_and_Figures-311] With its main campus in the Old Town, it has been ranked among the top 500 universities in the world by [QS World University Rankings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/QS_World_University_Rankings).[^cite_note-312] The university participates in projects with UNESCO and [NATO](21133.md). It has master's programs in English and Russian,[^cite_note-313] and programs in cooperation with other universities throughout Europe. The university has 14 faculties.[^cite_note-Facts_and_Figures-311]

Other universities include [Mykolas Romeris University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mykolas_Romeris_University),[^cite_note-314] Vilnius Gediminas Technical University[^cite_note-315] and the [Lithuanian University of Educational Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_University_of_Educational_Sciences), which merged with [Vytautas Magnus University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vytautas_Magnus_University) in 2018.[^cite_note-316] Specialized tertiary schools with university status include the [General Jonas Žemaitis Military Academy of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Jonas_%C5%BDemaitis_Military_Academy_of_Lithuania), the Lithuanian Academy of Music and Theatre, and the [Vilnius Academy of Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Academy_of_Arts). The museum associated with the [Vilnius Academy of Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Academy_of_Arts) contains about 12,000 artworks.[^cite_note-317]

### Libraries

A long, ornate reading room

A 16th-century central [Vilnius University Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_University_Library) reading room, decorated in 1803 with portraits of the 12 most prominent figures in antiquity, art and science

[^cite_note-318]

The Vilnius city municipality central library (Lithuanian: *Vilniaus miesto savivaldybės centrinė biblioteka*) operates [public libraries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_library) in the city.[^cite_note-319] It has 16 branches, one (Saulutė) dedicated to [children's literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Children's_literature).[^cite_note-320] Many libraries offer free [computer literacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computer_literacy) courses.[^cite_note-321] The public libraries require a free LIBIS (integrated information system of Lithuanian libraries) card.[^cite_note-322]

The [Martynas Mažvydas National Library of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martynas_Ma%C5%BEvydas_National_Library_of_Lithuania) (Lithuanian: *Lietuvos nacionalinė Martyno Mažvydo biblioteka*) in Gediminas Avenue, founded in 1919, collects, organizes and preserves Lithuania's written cultural heritage, collects Lithuanian and foreign documents relevant to research and Lithuania's educational and cultural needs, and provides library services to the public.[^cite_note-323] By 1 January 2025, the library had 6,425,401 physical units of documents.[^cite_note-\:0-324]

The [Wroblewski Library of the Lithuanian Academy of Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wroblewski_Library_of_the_Lithuanian_Academy_of_Sciences) (Lithuanian: *Lietuvos mokslų akademijos Vrublevskių biblioteka*) is open to all.[^cite_note-mab-325] The library had 3,683,670 physical units of documents by 1 January 2025,[^cite_note-\:0-324] and 9,491 registered users.[^cite_note-326]

Every [Lithuanian university and college](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_universities_and_colleges_in_Lithuania) has a library for students, professors and [alumni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alumni). The [National Open Access Scientific Communication and Information Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_University_Library#National_Open_Access_Scientific_Communication_and_Information_Center) of Vilnius University (Lithuanian: *Vilniaus universiteto bibliotekos Mokslinės komunikacijos ir informacijos centras*) in Saulėtekis Valley opened in 2013 and has over 800 workplaces in an area of 14,043.61 m<sup>2</sup> (151,164.2 sq ft).[^cite_note-327][^cite_note-328] Central Vilnius University Library,[^cite_note-329] Vilnius Gediminas Technical University Library, Mykolas Romeris University Library, ISM University of Management and Economics Library, European Humanities University Library, and Kazimieras Simonavičius University Library are on their respective campuses in Vilnius.[^cite_note-330]

## Religion

See also: [List of churches in Vilnius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_churches_in_Vilnius)

| Religion | People | % |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Roman Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholicism_in_Lithuania) | 350,797 | 65.5% |
| [Eastern Orthodox](10186.md) | 47,827 | 8.9% |
| [Old Believers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Believers) | 5,593 | 1.0% |
| [Evangelical Lutheran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelical_Lutheran_Church_in_Lithuania) | 1,594 | 0.3% |
| [Evangelical Reformed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Evangelical_Reformed_Church) | 1,186 | 0.2% |
| [Sunni Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Lithuania) | 798 | 0.2% |
| [Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Jews) | 796 | 0.2% |
| [Greek Catholic](214903.md) | 167 | <0.1% |
| [Karaites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karaite_Judaism) | 139 | <0.1% |
| Other | 5,050 | 0.9% |
| None | 47,655 | 8.9% |
| No response | 74,029 | 13.8% |

A small brick church, surrounded by trees

Vilnius's [Church of Saint Nicholas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_Saint_Nicholas,_Vilnius), built before 1387, is Lithuania's oldest surviving Catholic church.

Lithuania's pre-Christian religion, centred on the forces of nature and personified by deities such as [Perkūnas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perk%C5%ABnas) (the thunder god), is experiencing increased interest. [Romuva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romuva_\(religion\)) established a Vilnius branch in 1991.[^cite_note-332]

By the 17th century, Vilnius was known as a city of numerous religions. In 1600, Samuel Lewkenor's book about cities with universities was published in London;[^cite_note-333] According to Lewkenor, Vilnius's population included Catholics, Orthodox, followers of John Calvin and Martin Luther, Jews and Tartar Muslims.

During that century, Vilnius had a reputation as a city unrivaled in Europe for its number and variety of churches. Robert Morden wrote in *Geography Rectified or a Description of the World* that no other city in the world could surpass Vilnius in the number of churches and temples except, perhaps, Amsterdam.[^cite_note-334][^cite_note-335]

Vilnius is the seat of the [Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Vilnius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Vilnius), housing major church institutions and the archdiocesan Vilnius Cathedral. A number of Christian [beatified](233724.md) people, [martyrs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyr#Christianity), [servants of God](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Servant_of_God) and [saints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint) are associated with the city. They include the [Franciscan martyrs of Vilnius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franciscan_martyrs_of_Vilnius), the Orthodox martyrs [Anthony, John, and Eustathius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony,_John,_and_Eustathius), Saint Casimir, [Josaphat Kuntsevych](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josaphat_Kuntsevych), [Andrew Bobola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Bobola), [Raphael Kalinowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raphael_Kalinowski), [Faustina Kowalska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faustina_Kowalska), and [Jurgis Matulaitis-Matulevičius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jurgis_Matulaitis-Matulevi%C4%8Dius). There are a number of Roman Catholic churches in the city, small monasteries, and religious schools. Church architecture includes Gothic, Renaissance, Baroque and [neoclassical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoclassical_architecture) styles, with examples of each in the Old Town. [Eastern Rite Catholicism](214903.md) has also had a presence in Vilnius since the [Union of Brest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Brest). The Baroque Basilian Gate is part of an Eastern Rite [monastery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monastery_of_the_Holy_Trinity,_Vilnius).

A large white church against a blue sky

The Orthodox [Cathedral of the Theotokos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_of_the_Theotokos,_Vilnius), built in the 14th century by Grand Duke [Algirdas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algirdas) for [Ruthenians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruthenians) in the city's Ruthenian quarter

[^cite_note-Darius-336]

Vilnius has had an [Eastern Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox) presence since the 12th century, and the [Russian Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church) [Monastery of the Holy Spirit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orthodox_Church_of_the_Holy_Spirit,_Vilnius) is near the [Gate of Dawn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gate_of_Dawn). [St. Paraskeva's Orthodox Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_St._Paraskeva,_Vilnius) in the Old Town was the site of the 1705 [baptism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baptism) of [Hannibal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abram_Petrovich_Gannibal), the great-grandfather of [Alexander Pushkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Pushkin), by Tsar [Peter the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_the_Great). Many [Old Believers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Believers), who split from the Russian Orthodox Church in 1667, settled in Lithuania; a Supreme Council of Old Believers is based in Vilnius. The Orthodox [Church of St. Constantine and St. Michael](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_St._Constantine_and_St._Michael) was built in 1913. A number of Protestant and other Christian denominations[^cite_note-337] are represented in Vilnius, notably [Lutheran](23371382.md) Evangelicals and [Baptists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baptists).

### Judaism and Karaism

See also: [History of the Jews in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Lithuania)

Large, cream-coloured synagogue

The [Choral Synagogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Choral_Synagogue_\(Vilnius\))

Known as "Yerushalayim D'Lita" (the Jerusalem of Lithuania), Vilnius had been a world centre for [Torah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torah) study and had a large Jewish population since the 18th century. A major scholar of Judaism and the [Kabbalah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabbalah) was Rabbi Eliyahu Kremer, known as the [Vilna Gaon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilna_Gaon), whose writings significantly influence Orthodox Jews. The [Vilna Shas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilna_Edition_Shas), the most widely used version of the [Talmud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talmud), was published in the city in 1886.[^cite_note-338] Jewish life in Vilnius was destroyed during the Holocaust, and a memorial stone dedicated to victims of [Nazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazism) genocide is in the centre of the former [Jewish Ghetto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilna_Ghetto) on present-day Mėsinių Street. The [Vilna Gaon Museum of Jewish History](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilna_Gaon_Museum_of_Jewish_History) is dedicated to the history of Lithuanian Jewish life. The site of [Vilnius's largest synagogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Synagogue_of_Vilna), built in the early 1630s, destroyed by Nazi Germany during its [occupation of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_occupation_of_Lithuania_during_World_War_II) and later demolished by Soviet authorities, was found by [ground-penetrating radar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ground-penetrating_radar) in June 2015.[^cite_note-339] Archaeologists began excavating the site in 2016, and that work continues as of July 2024.[^cite_note-340]

The [Karaites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karaite_Judaism) are a Jewish sect who migrated to Lithuania from the Crimea. Small in numbers, they have become more prominent since Lithuanian independence and have restored their [kenesas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenesa) (including the [Vilnius Kenesa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Kenesa)).[^cite_note-341]

### Pilgrimage sites

> It is safe to say that I have been in Vilnius all my life, at least since I became conscious. I was in Vilnius with thoughts and heart – one could say \[my] whole being. And so it stayed – and in Rome.

— [Pope John Paul II](23805.md) at the [Dominican Church of the Holy Spirit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominican_Church_of_the_Holy_Spirit) during his 1993 visit to Lithuania[^cite_note-342]

An ornate Baroque chapel

Interior of the Chapel of the [Gate of Dawn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gate_of_Dawn), with its eponymous painting

Since the 1387 Christianization of Lithuania, Vilnius has become a centre of Christianity in the country and a [pilgrimage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_pilgrimage) site. The Vilnius Pilgrimage Centre (Lithuanian: *Vilniaus piligrimų centras*) coordinates pilgrimages, assists with their preparation, and performs pilgrimage pastoral care.[^cite_note-343] A number of places in Vilnius are associated with [miracles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miracles_in_Christianity) or mark events significant to Christians, and the Chapel of the Gate of Dawn is visited by thousands of Christian pilgrims annually. The gates were initially part of the defensive Wall of Vilnius; they were given to the [Carmelites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carmelites) in the 16th century, who installed a [chapel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chapel) in the gates with a 17th-century Catholic painting: *[Our Lady of the Gate of Dawn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Our_Lady_of_the_Gate_of_Dawn)*. The painting was later decorated with [gold-plated silver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riza) and is associated with miracles and a legend.[^cite_note-pilgrimage-344]

A painting of Jesus

The first Divine Mercy painting by [Eugeniusz Kazimirowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugeniusz_Kazimirowski) (1934) at the [Divine Mercy Sanctuary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanctuary_of_the_Divine_Mercy,_Vilnius)

The [Sanctuary of the Divine Mercy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanctuary_of_the_Divine_Mercy,_Vilnius) is a pilgrimage site which has a [Divine Mercy image](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divine_Mercy_image). Vilnius was the birthplace of the [Divine Mercy devotion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divine_Mercy_\(Catholic_devotion\)) when Saint [Faustina Kowalska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faustina_Kowalska) began her mission under the guidance of [Michał Sopoćko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Micha%C5%82_Sopo%C4%87ko), her [spiritual director](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spiritual_director). The first Divine Mercy image was painted in 1934 by [Eugeniusz Kazimirowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugeniusz_Kazimirowski) under the supervision of Kowalska, and it hangs in the Divine Mercy Sanctuary in Vilnius. Adoration of the [Blessed Sacrament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blessed_Sacrament) takes place in the shrine around the clock.[^cite_note-pilgrimage-344] The House of St. Faustina, in Antakalnis' V. Grybo Street, is open to pilgrims.[^cite_note-345]

The [Church of St. Philip and St. Jacob](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_St._Philip_and_St._Jacob,_Vilnius), near Lukiškės Square, has the painting of the Mother of God of Lukiškės which has reportedly attracted miracles.[^cite_note-pilgrimage-344] The [icon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Icon), painted in the 15th or 16th century, is one of the country's oldest examples of [easel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Easel) painting.[^cite_note-346] It was brought by Grand Duchy of Lithuania artillery general Motiejus Korvinas Gosievskis from the Russo-Polish War. Since 1684, miracles have been reported at the Vilnius Dominican monastery related to the image which were published in a 1737 book, *Mystical Fountain* (Lithuanian: *Mistinis fontanas*). The icon was restored and returned to the [Dominicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominican_Order) in 2012.[^cite_note-347]

[Three Crosses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Crosses) is a monument in the city. According to a legend in the *[Bychowiec Chronicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bychowiec_Chronicle)*, fourteen [Franciscan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franciscan) friars were invited to Vilnius from [Podolia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Podolia) by [Petras Goštautas](https://lt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petras%20Go%C5%A1tautas%20\(1305\)).[^cite_note-gidzi-348] The friars preached the [gospel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gospel) and denigrated pagan Lithuanian gods; angry city residents burned the monastery and killed the fourteen friars. Seven were beheaded on Bleak Hill, and the other seven were [crucified](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crucifixion) and thrown into the Neris or Vilnia.[^cite_note-gidzi-348]

Painting of a church at the top of many steps

Verkiai Calvary c. 1840s, built in gratitude for victory in the Second Northern War

[Verkiai Calvary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Verkiai_Calvary) (or Vilnius Calvary), Lithuania's second-oldest [calvary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calvary_\(sanctuary\)), is in the neighborhood of [Verkiai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Verkiai). The calvary was built from 1662 to 1669 in gratitude for victory in the [Second Northern War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Northern_War) (1655–60).[^cite_note-349] The consecration ceremony of the [Stations of the Cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stations_of_the_Cross) took place for [Pentecost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentecost) on 9 June 1669.[^cite_note-350] The calvary includes 20 brick chapels, seven wooden gates and a brick one, and a bridge with a wood chapel.[^cite_note-351] The path ends at the [Church of the Discovery of the Holy Cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_the_Discovery_of_the_Holy_Cross,_Vilnius). All the chapels except the four closest to the church were destroyed by Soviet authorities overnight with [dynamite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dynamite) in 1962. The calvary was reconstructed from 1990 to 2002, and the chapels were consecrated on Pentecost in 2002.[^cite_note-352] Pilgrimages to the calvary are organized regularly with the clergy.[^cite_note-353]

The Church Heritage Museum (Lithuanian: *Bažnytinio paveldo muziejus*) contains city's the oldest and largest collection of liturgical artefacts in the Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Vilnius.[^cite_note-pilgrimage-344][^cite_note-354] Vilnius is the only city in the Baltic states with an [Apostolic Nunciature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apostolic_Nunciature_to_Lithuania), where Pope John Paul II and [Pope Francis](1687680.md) stayed during their visits to Lithuania, [Latvia](17514.md) and [Estonia](28222445.md).[^cite_note-355]

## Parks, squares and cemeteries

Three white stone crosses, surrounded by vegetation

The [Three Crosses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Crosses) in [Kalnai Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalnai_Park)

Almost half of Vilnius is covered by green space such as parks, public gardens, and nature reserves. The city has a number of lakes where residents and visitors swim and barbecue in the summer. Thirty lakes and 16 rivers cover 2.1 percent of Vilnius's area, some of which have sand beaches.

Vingis Park, the city's largest, hosted several large rallies during Lithuania's drive towards independence in the 1980s. Sections of the annual [Vilnius Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Marathon) are on public walkways along the Neris. The green area next to the White Bridge is a popular place to enjoy good weather, and has become a venue for several musical and film events.

Aerial view of a large square

[Town Hall Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Town_Hall,_Vilnius)

[Cathedral Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_Square,_Vilnius) in the Old Town is surrounded by a number of the city's most historic sites. [Lukiškės Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luki%C5%A1k%C4%97s_Square) is the largest, bordered by several government buildings: the [Lithuanian Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Lithuania\)), Ministry of Finance, Polish Embassy and the [Genocide Victims' Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Occupations_and_Freedom_Fights), where the [KGB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KGB) tortured and killed opponents of the communist regime. A large statue of [Vladimir Lenin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Lenin) in its centre was removed in 1991.[^cite_note-356] Town Hall Square has been a centre of trade fairs, celebrations and events, including the [Kaziukas Fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaziuko_mug%C4%97). The city's Christmas tree is displayed there. State ceremonies are often held in [Daukanto Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daukanto_Square), facing the Presidential Palace.

Town Hall Square has also been host to the Vilnius [Portal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portal_\(sculptures\)) since 2021, the first in the world.[^cite_note-357] Visitors can view the following locations for three minutes at a time:[^cite_note-358]

- [Lublin](74594.md), Poland

- [Dublin](8504.md), Ireland

- [Ipswich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ipswich), United Kingdom

- [Philadelphia](50585.md), United States

Aerial view of a large green area with many trees

Bernardinai Garden

[Bernardinai Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernardinai_Garden), near [Gediminas Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gediminas'_Tower) (previously known as Sereikiškės Park), opened on 20 October 2013 after it was restored to its 19th-century Vladislovas Štrausas environment.[^cite_note-359] It is a venue for concerts, festivals, and exhibitions. [Chiune Sugihara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiune_Sugihara) [Sakura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sakura) Park was established in 2001, and a [Japanese garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_garden) (both in Šnipiškės) was opened in 2023.[^cite_note-360][^cite_note-361]

[Rasos Cemetery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rasos_Cemetery), consecrated in 1801, is the burial site of [Jonas Basanavičius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonas_Basanavi%C4%8Dius) and other [signatories of the 1918 Act of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Signatories_of_the_Act_of_Independence_of_Lithuania) and the heart of Polish leader [Józef Piłsudski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%B3zef_Pi%C5%82sudski). Two of the city's three [Jewish cemeteries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_cemeteries_of_Vilnius) were destroyed by communist authorities during the Soviet era, and the remains in the Vilna Gaon were moved to the remaining one. A monument was erected at the site of Užupis Old Jewish Cemetery was.[^cite_note-362] The [Bernardine Cemetery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernardine_Cemetery), established in 1810, has about 18,000 burials; closed during the 1970s, it is being restored. [Antakalnis Cemetery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antakalnis_Cemetery), established in 1809, has memorials to Polish, Lithuanian, German and Russian soldiers and the graves of those who were killed during the January Events.

## Tourism

A group of tourists in cool weather

Tourists in the Old Town

Aerial view of an urban neighborhood with some trees

[Užupis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U%C5%BEupis), a self-proclaimed republic, has [Bohemian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bohemianism) culture and art.

According to [Lithuanian Department of Statistics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Department_of_Statistics), 1,200,858 visitors rented rooms in Vilnius in 2018 and spent a total of 2,212,109 nights there; this was a respective increase of 12 percent and 11 percent over the previous year.[^cite_note-tourism2018-363] Eighty-one percent of the visitors were foreigners (970,577), 11 percent more than in 2017. Most foreign visitors (47 percent) came from Belarus (102,915), [Germany](11867.md) (101,999), Poland (99,386), Russia (90,388) and Latvia (61,829).[^cite_note-tourism2018-363] Nineteen percent of the guests were Lithuanian, 18 percent more than in 2017.[^cite_note-tourism2018-363]

A 2018 Vilnius visitor survey reported that 48 percent were visiting the city for the first time, 85 percent of tourists planned the trip by themselves, and 15 percent used a travel agency.[^cite_note-364] Forty percent said that they visited Vilnius to learn about the city's history and heritage, with 23 percent also planning trips to other parts of Lithuania.[^cite_note-365] Many Belarusians (about 200,000 [travel visas](330265.md) annually) visit the city's [shopping malls](235723.md) and submit half-meter-long receipts to [customs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Customs) officials.[^cite_note-366]

Vilnius's Tourist Information Centres were visited by 119,136 visitors in 2018 (95,932 foreigners and 23,204 Lithuanians), a five-percent increase over 2017.[^cite_note-tourism2018-363] The city's highest-rated tourist services are restaurants ([cafés](370815.md)), old-town attractions, hotels or other accommodations, trips to [Trakai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trakai), parks and other green zones, connections to [Vilnius Čiurlionis International Airport](166489.md), and food in hotels, restaurants and cafés.[^cite_note-367] Vilnius is one of a few European capitals which allows [hot air ballooning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot_air_ballooning) through the city, with nearly 1,000 trips in 2022.[^cite_note-368] In the City Costs Barometer 2019, Vilnius was ranked number one of European capitals for offering the best value to visitors.[^cite_note-369] The controversial [Vilnius Palace of Concerts and Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Palace_of_Concerts_and_Sports), built by Soviet authorities on the site of a Jewish graveyard, was scheduled to become the leading convention center in the Baltic states in 2022.[^cite_note-370]

### Hotels

Exterior of a large, old building

The Kempinski Hotel

Lithuania is a member of the [European Hotelstars Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hotel_rating#European_Hotelstars_Union).[^cite_note-371] Vilnius has six [five-star hotels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hotel_rating), all in the Old Town,[^cite_note-372] and 27 four-star hotels.[^cite_note-373] The [Kempinski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kempinski) Hotel, with a view of Cathedral Square, is considered the city's most luxurious hotel.[^cite_note-imf-374][^cite_note-375]

According to a 2018 Vilnius visitors' survey, 44 percent stayed in mid-range hotels (three or four stars), 12 percent stayed in standard or economy hotels (one or two stars) and 11 percent stayed in five-star hotels.[^cite_note-376] The city had 82 hotels, eight motels and 40 other accommodation facilities in 2019, with 6,822 rooms and 15,248 beds. The highest hotel-room occupancy was in August, and the lowest was in February.[^cite_note-tourism2018-363]

## Sports

A large group of young people in green T-shirts

2015 Vilnius Marathon volunteers

Downtown spectators, on foot and on bicycles

The 2024 CEV Beach Volley Nations Cup tournament was held in the central business district.

[^cite_note-377]

Several basketball teams are based in the city. [BC Wolves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BC_Wolves) began competing in the [2022–23 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022%E2%80%9323_LKL_season) of the [Lithuanian Basketball League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lietuvos_krep%C5%A1inio_lyga) (LKL). The largest team is [BC Rytas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BC_Rytas), who participates in the international [Basketball Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basketball_Champions_League) (BCL) and the LKL; they won the ULEB Cup (predecessor to the [EuroCup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EuroCup_Basketball)) in [2005](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004%E2%80%9305_ULEB_Cup) and the EuroCup in [2009](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008%E2%80%9309_Eurocup_Basketball). Their home arena is the 2,500-seat [Jeep Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeep_Arena); all European matches and important domestic matches are played at the 10,000-seat [Twinsbet Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twinsbet_Arena).

Vilnius also has several football teams; [FK Žalgiris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FK_%C5%BDalgiris), the main team, plays at the 5,000-seat [LFF Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LFF_Stadium).[^cite_note-378] The multi-use [Lithuania National Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuania_National_Stadium) is under construction. The 28-court [SEB Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SEB_Arena) is the largest [tennis](29773.md) complex in [central Europe](5188.md) and home of the Lithuanian tennis and [squash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Squash_\(sport\)) teams.[^cite_note-379]

[Olympic](27566.md) swimming champions [Lina Kačiušytė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lina_Ka%C4%8Diu%C5%A1yt%C4%97) and [Robertas Žulpa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robertas_%C5%BDulpa) are from Vilnius. The city has several public [swimming pools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swimming_pool), with the Lazdynai Swimming Pool the only [Olympic-size swimming pool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic-size_swimming_pool).[^cite_note-380] Vilnius is home to the Lithuanian [Bandy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandy) Association, Badminton Federation, Canoeing Sports Federation, Baseball Association, Biathlon Federation, Sailors Union, Football Federation, Fencing Federation, Cycling Sports Federation, Archery Federation, Athletics Federation, Ice Hockey Federation, Basketball Federation, Curling Federation, Rowing Federation, Wrestling Federation, Speed Skating Association, Gymnastics Federation, Equestrian Union, Modern Pentathlon Federation, Shooting Union, Triathlon Federation, Volleyball Federation, Tennis Union, Taekwondo Federation, Weightlifting Federation, Table Tennis Association, Skiing Association, Rugby Federation, and Swimming Federation.[^cite_note-381] The annual international [Vilnius Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Marathon) has thousands of participants.[^cite_note-382]

## Transport

A large, white building, seen from a parking lot

Vilnius Čiurlionis International Airport main entrance

Navigability of the Neris is limited; no regular water routes exist outside of Vilnius, although it was used for transport in the past.[^cite_note-383] Local transport on the river in Vilnius started in 2025 with electric boats.[^cite_note-384] [Vilnius Čiurlionis International Airport](166489.md), Lithuania's largest, serves about 50 cities in 25 countries.[^cite_note-385] The airport, 5 km (3.1 mi) from the city centre, has a direct link to the [Vilnius railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_railway_station). The station is a rail hub with direct passenger service to [Minsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minsk), [Kaliningrad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaliningrad), [Moscow](19004.md) and [Saint Petersburg](24320051.md), and is part of the [Pan-European Corridor IX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-European_Corridor_IX)'s Branch B.

Vilnius is the starting point of the [A1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A1_highway_\(Lithuania\)) motorway which runs across Lithuania, connecting its three major cities (Vilnius, Kaunas and [Klaipėda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klaip%C4%97da)), and is part of [European route E85](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_route_E85). The [A2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A2_highway_\(Lithuania\)), connecting Vilnius and [Panevėžys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panev%C4%97%C5%BEys), is part of the [E272](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_route_E272). Other highways out of the city include the [A3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A3_highway_\(Lithuania\)), [A4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A4_highway_\(Lithuania\)), [A14](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A14_highway_\(Lithuania\)), [A15](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A15_highway_\(Lithuania\)), and [A16](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A16_highway_\(Lithuania\)). Vilnius's southern bypass is the [A19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A19_highway_\(Lithuania\)).

### Public transport service

The bus, [trolleybus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trolleybuses_in_Vilnius) and boat networks are operated by Vilniaus viešasis transportas. JUDU (SĮ Susisiekimo paslaugos) is the public mobility agency responsible for planning, managing, and ticketing.[^cite_note-386] There are 125 bus, 16 trolleybus, six rapid bus, 9 night bus routes and one boat route.[^cite_note-387][^cite_note-388] The trolleybus network is one of Europe's most extensive; over 250 buses and 260 trolleybuses transport about 500,000 passengers every workday.[^cite_note-389] The first bus routes were established in 1926, and the first trolleybuses were introduced in 1956.[^cite_note-390]

At the end of 2007, an electronic monthly ticket system was introduced in which passengers could buy an electronic card in shops and newsstands and load it with money; monthly e-ticket cards could also be loaded over the Internet. Paper monthly tickets were in use until August 2008.[^cite_note-391] On 15 August 2012, e-cards were replaced by Vilnius Citizen Cards (Vilniečio Kortelė) which could be purchased at newsstands and loaded with money and ticket type. In 2014, a mobile app was introduced for public-transport tickets.[^cite_note-392]

Buses are low-floor [Volvo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volvo_Buses) and [Mercedes-Benz buses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-Benz_buses), and trolleybuses are manufactured by [Solaris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solaris_Bus_&_Coach). Older [Škoda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0koda_Works) vehicles, built in the Czech Republic and many refurbished, are still in service. In 2004, a contract was signed with Volvo Buses to purchase 90 new 7700 buses over a three-year period.[^cite_note-393]

In 2017, Vilnius began the largest upgrade of its bus service by purchasing 250 new low-floor buses. Sixty percent of the city's public buses were new by mid-2018, with free Wi-Fi and chargers for electronic devices.[^cite_note-394] On 5 September 2017, 50 new [Isuzu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isuzu) buses were introduced.[^cite_note-395] Vilnius City Municipality accepted bids for 41 new [trolleybuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trolleybus); Solaris contracted to provide the trolleybuses by autumn 2018, with free Wi-Fi and chargers.[^cite_note-396] On 13 November of that year, the municipality signed a contract with Solaris for the remaining 150 fourth-generation [Solaris Urbino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solaris_Urbino) buses (100 standard and 50 [articulated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Articulated_bus)) with free Wi-Fi and USB charging.[^cite_note-397] Five electric [Karsan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karsan) Jest Electric buses were introduced on 20 September 2019 for the number 89 route's narrow streets.[^cite_note-398]

[Vilnius Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Metro) and an electric tram have been proposed.[^cite_note-399] In 2018, the Seimas and the president approved a metro project.[^cite_note-400]

In June 2025, JUDU launched their first boat network, and thus using their first electric riverboat that runs on the river [Neris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neris). The ferry has stops at Vilnius' most notable bridges like [Žvėrynas Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%BDv%C4%97rynas_Bridge), [White Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Bridge_\(Vilnius\)), [Green Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Bridge_\(Vilnius\)), and [Mindaugas Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mindaugas_Bridge).[^cite_note-401] Boats were built by the Latvian company SIA "BIC Group".[^cite_note-402]

  [Solaris Urbino 18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solaris_Urbino_18) bus and Škoda 26Tr Solaris [trolleybuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trolleybus) in Vilnius

### Electric vehicle infrastructure

Vilnius is the city with the most electric vehicles in Lithuania.[^cite_note-403] The city has tens of public high-power charging stations, provided by a state-owned enterprise [Ignitis ON](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lietuvos_Energija) and a municipal enterprise Susisiekimo paslaugos.[^cite_note-404][^cite_note-405] Vilnius city municipality and the Government of Lithuania encourages the usage of electric vehicles and has granted a number of benefits for such cars users (e.g. six charging stations offers a completely free charging in Vilnius, free parking in the city's public areas,[^cite_note-406] electric vehicles are allowed to drive in a separate *A* road lane and significantly benefits in the traffic jams,[^cite_note-407] electric and [hybrid vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hybrid_vehicle) [license plates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/License_plate) begins with a letter E).[^cite_note-408]

## Healthcare

When Vilnius was part of the Grand Duchy of Lithuania, the city had [public bathhouses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_bathhouse); one-fourth the city's houses had individual bathhouses, and almost half had alcohol distilleries.[^cite_note-409] In 1518, doctor and [canon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canon_\(priest\)) Martynas Dušnickis established the first *špitolė* in Vilnius: Lithuania's first hospital-like institution which treated people unable to care for themselves due to health, age, or poverty.[^cite_note-410]

The [Brotherhood of Saint Roch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brotherhood_of_Saint_Roch) maintained basic hospitals and shelters for the sick and disabled in Vilnius from 1708 to 1799, although it is unknown if the brothers had any medical education. They hired [paramedics](75006.md), doctors, [surgeons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surgeon), and female [nurses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nurse) for female patients. A significant number of patients had [sexually transmitted diseases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexually_transmitted_diseases) which other Catholic hospitals refused to treat. The brotherhood sheltered pregnant women, [abandoned children](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abandoned_children) and patients with injuries, [tuberculosis](30653.md), [rheumatism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rheumatism) and [arthritis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthritis).[^cite_note-411]

In 1805, the [Vilnius Medical Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Medical_Society) was established by [Joseph Frank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Frank_\(physician\)) (son of [Johann Peter Frank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johann_Peter_Frank)) as the first medical society in eastern Europe.[^cite_note-412] The same year, the society established a [teaching hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teaching_hospital) ([clinic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clinic)) as part of the Vilnius University Faculty of Medicine.[^cite_note-413][^cite_note-FirstClinic-414] From 1918 to 1941, the [Lithuanian Sanitary Aid Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Sanitary_Aid_Society) operated in Vilnius.[^cite_note-415]

The [Ministry of Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Health_\(Lithuania\)), in Vilnius, is responsible for Lithuanian [health care](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_care).[^cite_note-416] Vilnians pay compulsory [health insurance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_insurance) (6.98 percent of their salary), which is governed by the Vilnius Territorial Health Insurance Fund and guarantees free health care to every insured person. Some residents, such as the disabled, children and full-time students, are exempt from the tax.[^cite_note-417]

[Vilnius University Hospital Santaros Klinikos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_University_Hospital_Santaros_Klinikos) and the Vilnius City Clinical Hospital are the city's primary [hospitals](18994221.md).[^cite_note-418][^cite_note-419] Vilnius also has eight [polyclinics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polyclinic), the Medical Centre of the Ministry of the Interior, and a number of private health-care facilities.[^cite_note-420]

## Media

Title page of a 1760 newspaper

Title page of *Kurier Litewski* (1760)

The first Lithuanian weekly newspaper, *[Kurier Litewski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurier_Litewski)*, was published in Vilnius from 1760 to 1763.[^cite_note-421] Vilnius is home to a number of newspapers, magazines and other publications, including *[Lietuvos rytas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lietuvos_rytas)*, *[Lietuvos žinios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lietuvos_%C5%BEinios)*, *[Verslo žinios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Verslo_%C5%BEinios)*, *[Respublika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Respublika_\(Lithuanian_newspaper\))*, *Valstiečių laikraštis*, *Mokesčių žinios*, *Aktualijos*, *[15min](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/15min)*, *[Vilniaus diena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilniaus_diena)*, *Vilniaus Kraštas*, *[Lietuvos aidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lietuvos_aidas)*, *[Valstybė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valstyb%C4%97)*, *[Veidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veidas)*, *Panelė*, the Franciscan *[Bernardinai.lt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernardinai.lt)*, the Russian *Litovskij kurjer* and the Polish *Tygodnik Wileńszczyzny*.[^cite_note-422]

The [Vilnius TV Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_TV_Tower) in [Karoliniškės](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karolini%C5%A1k%C4%97s) broadcasts to the city.[^cite_note-423] The most-viewed networks in Lithuania are headquartered in Vilnius and include [LRT televizija](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LRT_televizija), [TV3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TV3_Lithuania), [LNK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LNK_\(television_station\)), [BTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BTV_\(Lithuania\)), [LRT Plius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LRT_Plius), [LRT Lituanica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LRT_Lituanica), [TV6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TV6_\(Lithuania\)), [Lietuvos rytas TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lietuvos_rytas_TV), [TV1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TV1_\(Lithuania\)), [TV8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TV8_\(Lithuania\)), [Sport1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport1_\(Lithuania\)), [Liuks!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liuks!), [Info TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Info_TV).[^cite_note-424]

Very tall transmission tower, surrounded by smaller buildings

[Vilnius TV Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_TV_Tower)

Vilnius's first [radio station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_station), *Rozgłośnia Wileńska*, began broadcasting in the [Žvėrynas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%BDv%C4%97rynas) microdistrict on 28 November 1927 and was moved to present-day Gediminas Avenue in 1935.[^cite_note-425] [M-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M-1_\(Lithuanian_radio_station\)), the country's first commercial radio station, began broadcasting from Vilnius in 1989. Other Lithuanian or foreign-language radio stations also broadcast from Vilnius, most from the Vilnius TV Tower or the [Vilnius Press House](https://lt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilniaus%20spaudos%20r%C5%ABmai).[^cite_note-426] The Lithuanian Union of Journalists (Lithuanian: *Lietuvos žurnalistų sąjunga*) and the Lithuanian Society of Journalists (Lithuanian: *Lietuvos žurnalistų draugija*) are headquartered in Vilnius.[^cite_note-427][^cite_note-428]

[^cite_note-5]: &#x20;This nickname was very popular among the [Lithuanian nobility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_nobility), citizens of Vilnius, and poets, especially during the Baroque period. Many poets of the period, including [Maciej Kazimierz Sarbiewski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maciej_Kazimierz_Sarbiewski), called Vilnius "the capital of Palemon" or "the city of Palemon". Živilė Nedzinskaitė, Vilnius XVII–XVIII a. LDK lotyniškojoje poezijoje, Acta Academiae Artium Vilnensis, Vilnius, 2010, p. 16; Eugenija Ulčinaitė, Motiejus Kazimieras Sarbievijus: Antikos ir krikščionybės sintezė; Vilniaus pasveikinimas, Lietuvių literatūros ir tautosakos institutas, Vilnius, 2001, pp. 47, 59, 61, 63; etc.
